## 19. Step D screens (rules 40-53), one line each
- **40 Formatting and placement:** no bold, underline, capitals, type-size, separate-document or initialing rule for residential lease terms (battery 5). Prescribed wording lives in notices and forms outside the lease, except the § 67-5909(8)(h) restoration term (§4).
- **41 Just-cause:** none (`edu-no-just-cause-id`). A fixed term ends without a notice to quit (§ 6-303(1)), but § 55-304(2) requires 30 days' written notice of nonrenewal. `surrender-end-of-term-ks-ne` does not replace that notice, and its ID note says so.
- **42 Required text inside a shared clause:** § 67-5909(8)(h): the restoration provision 'shall be included in any lease or rental agreement'. Carried by `disability-modification-restoration-id` (REQUIRED), beside `no-alterations`.
- **43 Cure promises:** `default-by-tenant`'s carve-out preserves the § 6-303 notices, including the no-notice case for a breach that cannot be performed and the notice-to-quit grounds. The base `early-termination`'s 10-day cure was not tagged.
- **44 Terms turned into duties:** § 6-320(a)(5) makes any lease term 'materially affecting the health and safety of the tenant' enforceable by the tenant's suit, with possible treble damages. `landlord-maintenance` matches the statute's own duties, so nothing extra is promised. § 55-304(1) and § 55-305(2)(b)(ii) notice changes are preserved by `entire-agreement`.
- **45 Electronic notices:** the Idaho UETA excludes only wills and most of the UCC (§ 28-50-103), but no statute names email as a service method for § 6-303 or § 6-320(d) notices (battery 4: 0 hits). No electronic-notice clause offered (`edu-notice-service-id`).
- **46 Lease as the notice:** no Idaho provision lets the lease serve as, or waive, a § 6-303 notice. § 55-212 lets a lease's right of reentry support an ordinary action for possession without notice once the right accrues; the library's default clause makes the right accrue only after written notice, so no clause relies on it (§6.1).
- **47 Knowing-use penalties:** none for lease terms. § 18-5812A penalizes intentionally denying housing to a service-dog user or charging extra; `assistance-animal-accommodation` complies.
- **48 Separate documents:** none required for residential leases (battery 5).
- **49 Collection costs:** no ban. § 26-2229A(4)(c) conditionally permits agency fees 'expressly authorized by the agreement creating the debt', offered as `collection-fee-id`. `default-by-tenant`'s fee sentence is consistent with § 6-324.
- **50 'Lease controls' wording:**
  - § 6-321(2): refund time 'if no time is fixed by agreement'. Fixed at 30 days on purpose (`security-deposit-return-id`).
  - § 6-321(1): deductions for 'the contingencies specified in the deposit arrangement'. `security-deposit-use` is that specification.
  - § 28-22-104(1): the 12% legal rate unless a written contract fixes another. `unpaid-amounts-interest-id` (optional, capped at 12%).
  - § 28-22-105: a set dishonored-check fee 'under a written agreement'. `returned-payments-id`.
  - § 6-320(a)(2): facilities 'supplied by the landlord'. `appliances-included` fixes what is supplied.
  - § 55-305(2): charges only as the agreement states. Every ID fee row states its amount or a bracket.
- **51 Plain-language and consumer statutes:** no plain-language statute (battery 6). The Consumer Protection Act reaches leasing and renting of real property (§ 48-602(2), (6)); its unconscionability factors (§ 48-603C) and its blank-space and copy-at-signing rules (§ 48-603(12)-(13), reach unsettled) are in `edu-consumer-protection-act-id`. No statement of waivers is required.
- **52 Exculpation:** no voiding statute (battery 3: 0 hits). The ks-oh-ca and ks-oh variants are used (§2.1).
- **53 Figures against shared clauses:** `returned-payments` ceiling wording replaced (`returned-payments-id`, $20 set-fee bracket); base `holdover` 'maximum permitted' replaced (`holdover-ca`); base `parking-vehicle-rules` towing for expired registration replaced (`parking-vehicle-rules-id`, § 49-1806(3)); `late-fee` has no Idaho figure to conflict with. `guest-policy-day-limit`'s 14 days conflicts with nothing (no guest statute).

---

## Proposed SOP changes
1. **Check whether the state's bulk or chapter downloads lag its section pages, and if they do, overlay every section in the session's official 'code sections affected' list before searching.** Reason: Idaho's chapter PDFs omit 2026 amendments that the section pages print (§ 6-310A); a whole-code search built on the PDFs alone would have searched stale text for about 1,170 sections.
2. **Test every absence pattern against a positive written in the state's own number style (for example 'one (1) year', 'thirty (30) days').** Reason: Idaho writes numbers with a parenthetical numeral; the first statute-of-frauds pattern returned zero because of it and would have produced a false absence.
3. **Run the rule-40 formatting battery before drafting, not at the end, and include the state's consumer-protection list of unfair practices in it.** Reason: in Idaho the formatting battery, run last, surfaced § 48-603(12)-(13) (blank spaces; copy at signing), which bears on how every lease is signed and on the library's bracket convention.
4. **Before relying on a real lease's text again, save the extracted text itself (not only its hash) to the source folder.** Reason: the Idaho provision map had to re-extract the BYU-Idaho contract in a later session because only the hash and a summary were saved; the second extraction's character count differed because of the join method, which a saved copy would have avoided.

## Proposed topic questions
- `disability-accommodation`: Must the lease itself include the landlord's right to condition a disability modification on restoration (a state fair housing act copying 42 U.S.C. § 3604(f)(3)(A) plus a 'shall be included in any lease' sentence)?
- `towing`: Does state towing law bar towing or booting a vehicle solely for expired or improper registration, or require signage naming the towing firm?
- `required-fees`: Must every fee, fine, interest or other charge be stated in the written lease, with a notice period to add one later?
- `rent-increase-notice`: Does the notice period apply to fixed-term leases as well as periodic ones, and is there a separate nonrenewal-notice duty?
- `consumer-protection-act`: Does the state's consumer protection act list blank spaces at signing or failing to give a copy at signing as unlawful practices, and does it reach residential leases?
- `unpaid-damages-interest`: Does a fee statute require interest on late amounts to be stated in the lease before it can be charged, even where a legal rate applies without a contract?
- `unauthorized-occupant-removal`: Is there a sheriff-run removal for squatters with a statutory complaint form, and what liability attaches to misuse?
- `security-deposit-return`: Does the statute let the lease fix the refund time within a range ('if no time is fixed by agreement')?

**Propagation note, 2026-09-30 (rule 62):** `early-termination-ks`, which this state is tagged on, gained one sentence: the early-termination option and fee apply only if the lease has a fixed Term; a periodic tenancy ends on the notice that law and the lease provide, without a fee. Uniform edit by Claude Code, proposed by SC's retro (AL retro finding 4). Nothing the landlord has under law is removed.

## Propagated from the Wyoming retro, 2026-10-01

1. **Shared-row edit (Claude Code, Taylor's approval) — `appliances-included`.** "which Landlord will maintain as described in this Lease's Maintenance & Repairs Section" now reads "which Landlord will maintain as provided in this Lease and applicable law". Driver: the WY retro (WY log §9 item 2) found the pointer named a section that seven states (WY, KS, NE, MN, ND, SD, OH) no longer have. Recorded as **uniform** (rule 62): the promise to maintain the listed items is unchanged, and the new wording names no section, so it can't dangle again. This state's lease keeps a Maintenance & Repairs section, which is still part of 'this Lease', so nothing changes in substance here. `last_checked` reset to 2026-10-01.

## Propagated shared-row edit, 2026-10-02 (Taylor, at the Michigan sync)

Not a re-audit; nothing else in this state was reviewed.

**Propagation note (uniform edit, rule 62): `snow-removal` rewritten.** Old: 'Unless Landlord provides snow removal service, Tenant is responsible for prompt, reasonable removal of snow and ice from any walkway, driveway, porch, or entrance at the property that Tenant uses, to help keep those areas safe and passable.' New: 'Unless Landlord provides snow removal, Tenant will promptly remove snow and ice from the areas of the property Tenant uses for walking, parking and access. This does not include areas shared with other residents.' Why: Taylor found the list of areas too specific (properties differ, and a list invites arguments about what it covers), and Michigan's sync showed the clause should say outright that shared areas stay with the landlord. The edit only narrows the tenant's duty; this state's existing note on the row still holds.

## Circle-back checks (SOP 1.38), 2026-10-03

**Date:** 2026-10-03 · circle-back in ID's chat (rule 8) · Opus, high effort; research mode not used (no rule-9 trigger: the checks ran on the official Idaho Code and Constitution pages in the built-in browser). **Input:** `lease-clauses.csv`, 2,876 rows, as the prompt says; ID 121 active. Old output files deleted first (`lease-clauses-ID-delta.csv`, `lease-clause-decision-log-ID.md`, both from 2026-09-30). The attached files are the only source of truth; they agree with the earlier work in this chat and add the two later shared edits recorded at the end of ID's log (`appliances-included`, `snow-removal`). Scalpel, not a re-audit (rule 1): only the five items below were opened. Search records are saved in `sources/batteries/retro-2026-10-03.md`.

**Rule 79 basis for this pass:** the saved section files from 2026-09-30 for §§ 6-201, 6-301 to 6-304, 6-316, 6-317, 6-320, 6-324, 12-120, 26-2229A and 55-305 were compared with the live section pages before reuse. The content is identical after lowercasing and collapsing whitespace. A byte hash differs only because the pages now render headings in capitals and drop the blank line before 'History:'.

1. **Rule 54t (tenant-caused damage): fixed, with 1 optional clause and 1 education row added.**
   - **Not previously covered.** ID's log records no 54t screen; the SOP conformance note marked it due. The casualty row was checked first: `casualty-termination-id` already gives no termination or abatement for a casualty 'caused by Tenant, members of Tenant's household, or Tenant's guests'.
   - **What was read:**
     - § 6-201: waste; 'there may be judgment for treble damages'.
     - § 6-303(4): waste contrary to the lease terminates it on a three-day notice to quit.
     - § 6-320(a), (d): the tenant's action for damages and specific performance. Its text has no tenant-fault exception.
     - § 6-311: a rent bond for a detainer continuance, not a tenant remedy.
     - § 55-305(2), (4).
   - **What was searched:** every chapter of Titles 6, 9, 29, 41 and 55 (143 chapter PDFs) for accidental-fire, destruction or untenantability, rent-abatement, rebuild and tenant-fault wording. There was no residential-tenancy hit.
   - **Each abatement or exit provision, checked separately:**
     - Idaho has no casualty statute, no essential-services remedy, no statutory landlord-breach termination and no rent-into-court remedy.
     - So there is no fault exception to find. A no-abatement term waives no statutory right.
   - **What Idaho law gives the landlord:**
     - Repair cost: as damages (`tenant-maintenance`), and waste with possible treble damages.
     - Rent during repairs: it runs under the lease unless the lease abates it.
     - Lost rent if the lease ends: contract damages. There is no statutory mitigation rule, and the measure is case law, not read.
     - The tenant has no statutory walk-away right.
   - **Rows:**
     - New `tenant-caused-damage-id` (CONDITIONAL, SERVES_LANDLORD): the TN model, which is not tagged, plus a last sentence preserving the tenant's Idaho repair action.
     - New `edu-tenant-caused-damage-id`.
     - `casualty-termination-id`: ID note and `last_checked` only, adding a pointer to the two new rows.

2. **Rule 35c (Constitution screen): checked, no issue; no row changed.**
   - **Not previously covered.** ID's log §1.3 says the Constitution was not searched.
   - **Loaded:** all 21 articles from legislature.idaho.gov/statutesrules/idconst/ (234 sections, 155,803 characters, every section link loaded).
   - **Controls:** 'legislature' 102 sections and 'governor' 33; the nonsense term returned 0. Every pattern matched a synthetic positive first.
   - **Results:**
     - Cannabis or marijuana and controlled substances: **0**. Idaho's constitution has no cannabis provision, so the shared `smoking-policy` ban on smoking or vaping marijuana stands. This is the opposite of Missouri.
     - Arms: Art. I, § 11 restricts what 'law' may do (licensure, registration, confiscation), and no ID-tagged clause restricts firearms.
     - Speech: Art. I, § 9 ('Every person may freely speak, write and publish'). `common-area-use`'s consent rule for outside signs already yields to any display 'applicable law entitles Tenant to make'. Whether § 9 reaches a private landlord is case law, not read.
     - Search: Art. I, § 17 reaches unreasonable searches and seizures, which is state action.
     - Privacy: the only hit is crime victims' rights in the justice process (Art. I, § 22).
     - Hunting: Art. I, § 23 creates no right to trespass on private property.
     - Lease, tenant and residence: 24 hits, all on public lands, bonds, transportation, water or office residency. None reaches a residential lease.
   - **No initiated amendments to check.** Under Art. XX, § 1, amendments come only from the legislature with voter ratification; Art. III, § 1's initiative power is to propose laws.

3. **Targeted fix 3 (holdover rate, rule 54 as amended at the NE sync): checked; verdict unchanged, education row fixed.**
   - **The statutory measure is neither conditional nor a lump sum.**
     - § 6-317 lets the court treble 'the actual damages' whenever a landlord 'recovers damages for a ... detention'. It has no willful or bad-faith condition.
     - § 6-316(1) has the damages assessed for the whole unlawful detainer.
     - So the ordinary good-faith holdover still gets actual damages, normally the rental value, which `holdover-ca` claims. No gap is left.
   - **Verdict (log §6.1):** no holdover-rate clause, unchanged.
   - **Row fixed:** `edu-holdover-damages-id` now says a set rate is lawful as far as statute goes, why the library doesn't offer it, and that a landlord can add their own clause after taking advice. Whether a premium rate is a penalty is case law, not read. Before, the row stated the decision without saying the option was lawful (rule 54).

4. **Targeted fix 4 (rule 62 vetting, `default-by-tenant`, CO proposal to delete 'and reasonable costs and expenses'): checked; NOT vouched as a uniform edit for Idaho.**
   - **Idaho has no rule that needs the deletion.** No Idaho statute voids a one-way cost or fee provision in a residential lease.
     - § 6-324 makes attorney fees mandatory and mutual by statute in detainer actions, whatever the lease says. Treble-damages cases are excepted, and in nonpayment cases the fees depend on the three-day notice's advisory.
     - § 12-120 is mutual by its own terms.
     - Court-cost rules (Idaho R. Civ. P. 54) were not read.
   - **Deleting the phrase could give up two things.**
     - § 55-305(2)(b): for written agreements entered into or renewed from 2023-07-01, an owner may not charge a 'fee, fine, assessment, interest, or other cost ... not included in the rental agreement' without 30 days' written notice.
     - § 26-2229A(4)(c): a licensed collection agency may collect 'expenses incidental to the principal obligation' only if they are 'expressly authorized by the agreement creating the debt', or under one of the section's other grounds.
     - The clause's mutual closing sentence covers only court costs and fees 'incurred in connection with any legal proceedings'. Without the phrase, the Idaho lease would no longer mention pre-suit default costs in general (re-letting, cleaning, collection expenses).
     - Whether those count as an 'other cost' and whether the general wording is specific enough are both unsettled. The optional `collection-fee-id`, `keys` and `tenant-maintenance` cover parts of this.
   - **Recommendation:** Colorado's driver is Colorado-only, so make the change as a Colorado override, not a shared edit. If it is made shared, ID would need an override that keeps the phrase. Shared text not edited.

5. **Targeted fix 5 (rule 62 vetting, `default-by-tenant`, MN proposal to move the no-cure carve-out into its own sentence reaching both limbs): vouched, no change needed.**
   - § 6-303(2) always requires a three-day written notice requiring payment of the amount due, or possession, before nonpayment is an unlawful detainer. No Idaho law lets a landlord proceed on nonpayment without it.
   - So 'except where applicable law permits Landlord to proceed without giving Tenant an opportunity to cure' can't reach the rent limb in Idaho. It still covers the § 6-303(3) proviso (a breach that cannot afterward be performed), § 6-303(4) and § 6-303(5).
   - The drug-activity ground (§ 6-303(5)) and holdover (§ 6-303(1)) are separate grounds, not nonpayment.
   - Recorded here as the 'Vouches given' entry for ID's propagation section (rule 62). Shared text not edited.

**Rows changed** (`lease-clauses-ID-retro-delta.csv`, 4 rows, 17 columns, CRLF):
- **New:** `tenant-caused-damage-id` and `edu-tenant-caused-damage-id`.
- **Edited ID-only rows:** `edu-holdover-damages-id` (`bodyText`, notes) and `casualty-termination-id` (notes).
- **Integrity:** each row has `last_checked` 2026-10-03. No shared row changed, no ID lease clauses share a topic, every '§' is prefixed and no `{{variable}}` was added.
- **Counts:** ID goes from 121 to 123 active (65 lease clauses, 58 education). Every other state's count is unchanged.

### Proposed SOP changes
1. **Rule 79: compare saved section files with live pages by content, not by byte hash, when the site's rendering can change.** Lowercase both texts and collapse whitespace, or use `textContent`, before hashing. Reason: Idaho's section pages now render headings in capitals and drop a blank line, so every 2026-09-30 byte hash differed although the statute text was unchanged. A byte-only rule would have forced a needless re-read or a false 'changed' finding.

## Circle-back sync (Claude Code, 2026-10-03)

- **Merged** with `merge-delta.py --base 2b10851`: 2 new rows (`tenant-caused-damage-id`, `edu-tenant-caused-damage-id`) and 2 updated (`edu-holdover-damages-id`, `casualty-termination-id`); nothing refused. ID active 121 → 123.
- **Citations file:** rows added for the two new rows; `last_checked` on the two edited rows set to 2026-10-03.
- **Rule 62:**
  - ID vouched for MN's no-cure-sentence edit to `default-by-tenant`.
  - ID declined CO's deletion of "and reasonable costs and expenses" (Idaho Code § 55-305(2)(b), § 26-2229A(4)(c)). Because one tagged state can't vouch, the deletion went in at this sync as a Colorado override, `default-by-tenant-co`, as ID recommended. CO is untagged from the shared row, and the shared text keeps the phrase for Idaho and every other tagged state. The vetting question was removed from the circle-back folders that still carried it.
- **Guards:** all pass. **Statute spot-check, 3 of 3, on legislature.idaho.gov:** § 6-317 (treble damages, no willfulness condition); § 6-303(2) (three-day notice requiring payment or possession); § 55-305(2)(b) (no fee or other cost not in a written rental agreement without 30 days' written notice).
- **SOP 1.39:** ID's rule 79 proposal adopted (compare saved section files with live pages by normalised content, not raw bytes).

## Propagated shared-row edit, 2026-10-04 (at the NY circle-back sync)

- `default-by-tenant`: MN's proposal merged once every state tagged on `default-by-tenant` (18 states) and `default-by-tenant-co` (CO, NY) had vetted it, NY last. The no-cure carve-out moved out of the non-rent limb into its own sentence reaching both limbs, in the wording already merged on `default-by-tenant-ks-ne`: "Landlord need not give Tenant an opportunity to cure any breach, including a failure to pay Rent, where applicable law permits Landlord to proceed without one." It is self-limiting, so it reaches a breach only where ID law lets Landlord proceed without a cure opportunity. Uniform; no ID override.

## Cross-state decisions, 2026-10-04 (Taylor; central check by Claude Code)

- **New federal row:** `edu-cares-act-notice` is now tagged for ID. Under 15 U.S.C. § 9058(c), a landlord of a property with a federally backed mortgage, or in a covered federal housing program, may not require the tenant to vacate until 30 days after a notice to vacate; whether that still applies after the 2020 moratorium is unsettled. Any ID row that already mentions the CARES Act stays; the federal row is the library's standard explanation.
- **Shared-text edits merged centrally** (Taylor approved merging now; each only narrows the tenant's obligations or defers to applicable law, so it can't breach ID's law; to be confirmed at ID's end-of-run consistency pass):
- `smoking-policy`: Tenant pays for smoking damage caused by "Tenant, an occupant, or a guest or invitee of Tenant", no longer by anyone's smoking.
- `no-alterations`: the last sentence adds that the clause "does not change who owns an installation that applicable law makes Tenant's property".
- `addendum-precedence`: a disclosure, notice or addendum "that applicable law says controls over this Lease" now controls, as one the law requires already did.

# Topic gaps pass (end-of-run consistency pass, 2026-11-12): Desktop's gaps log

## Topic gaps: Idaho (end-of-run consistency pass): gaps log

State: Idaho (ID). Pass run 2026-11-12 under SOP 2.05, from GAPS-PROMPT.md staged 2026-11-09. Scope: the 247 topics in missing-topics-ID.md and Idaho items 1 to 12. Rows are added; no existing ID row or shared row text is edited, except shared rows tagged ID (states + an "ID: ..." notes segment). Proposed changes to existing rows are in §6 for Claude Code to apply at sync.

This file is cumulative and is redelivered after every batch; the status line below says how far the pass has got.

**Status: final (2026-11-12).** All eight batches canvassed; 247 of 247 topics have a row keyed to them; independent check complete (two rounds, §5); `check.py` 0 errors.

### §1 Sources, channel and currency
- **Statutes and Constitution:** corpus-ID-20261109 (built 2026-11-09 from legislature.idaho.gov only): the whole Idaho Code (74 titles, 1,453 chapters with sections, 22,745 sections; every chapter held whole) and the Idaho Constitution (234 sections). Re-verified 2026-11-12: `verify_id.py` 2,072 files, 0 mismatches against the manifest. Currency: the site states "current through the 2026 Legislative Session" (rechecked live 2026-11-12); no 2025 or 2026 extraordinary session. Section text is the live section page for every section touched by 2025 or 2026 legislation (2,027 sections) and the chapter PDF otherwise (PDF text prints ASCII quotes and `--`; wording identical).
- **Live cross-check, 2026-11-12 (browser, legislature.idaho.gov section pages):** Idaho Code § 6-303, Idaho Code § 6-320, Idaho Code § 6-321, Idaho Code § 29-110 (a chapter-PDF section), Idaho Code § 55-304 and Idaho Code § 55-305, each canonicalized (lowercase, whitespace collapsed, quotes and dashes folded, from "<number>.") and hashed: all six equal the corpus.
- **Channel for sources outside the corpus:** the shell cannot reach these sites (proxy), so each was read in the built-in browser, the text transcribed to a file, and the file's whitespace-collapsed SHA-256 checked against the hash computed in the page. This is weaker than a byte copy and is recorded as such in `fetched/REGISTRY.tsv`. Read whole this way:
  - FEDERAL (uscode.house.gov, current): 15 U.S.C. § 9058, 34 U.S.C. § 12491, 42 U.S.C. § 3604, 42 U.S.C. § 3617, 42 U.S.C. § 4852d, 50 U.S.C. § 3955.
  - FEDERAL (ecfr.gov, current eCFR, 2026-11-12): 24 C.F.R. § 982.308, § 982.310, § 982.404, § 982.405, § 982.451, § 982.453, § 982.510, § 982.551; 47 C.F.R. § 1.4000 (excerpt: (a), (b)).
  - FEDERAL (uscode.house.gov, current through Public Law 119-138, 2026-11-12), excerpts: 11 U.S.C. § 362(a), (b)(22), (b)(23), (l), (m) and 11 U.S.C. § 365(d)(1). The whole of each section was loaded in the page; only these parts were saved, and the registry marks them as excerpts.
  - Court rules (isc.idaho.gov printer pages, 2026-11-12): I.R.C.P. 4, I.R.C.P. 13, I.R.C.P. 17, I.R.C.P. 38 and I.R.C.P. 39, court rules. All 113 I.R.C.P. rules were also loaded and searched in the page (not saved whole). No rule covers confession of judgment, a stipulated judgment entered without action, or who may appear for an entity party.
  - Agency rule: IDAPA 31.21.01 (Public Utilities Commission, Utility Customer Relations Rules; PDF linked from adminrules.idaho.gov, served from files.dfm.idaho.gov), saved in three hash-matched parts after stripping private-use glyph characters (U+F020) the PDF text layer carries.
  - Agency rules, excerpts (whole chapter read in the page, parts relied on saved): IDAPA 18.08.01 (State Fire Marshal, International Fire Code adoption: rules 001, 029, 030, 056) and IDAPA 24.39.30 (Building Code Board: rule 600 adoptions and the residential-code sprinkler and alarm amendments).
  - IDAPA current-rules index (adminrules.idaho.gov/current-rules/, 2026-11-12): 44 agencies, 370 chapters, searched by title (not saved whole). There is no IDAPA 41 (public health districts) title.
- **Not reviewed:** case law, Attorney General opinions, city and county ordinances, local court rules, IDAPA rules other than those named, HUD forms. Rows say so where it matters.
- **Site access:** Taylor approved adminrules.idaho.gov, isc.idaho.gov and files.dfm.idaho.gov in the browser. No bot block or CAPTCHA was met.

### §4 Decisions and questions asked of Taylor
No question was put to Taylor in this pass so far; every point below was decided in the pass (rule 76: legal and drafting calls are Claude's).

**Reading of the fee statute used throughout.** Idaho Code § 55-305(2) bars a fee, fine, assessment, interest or other cost 'In an amount greater than that agreed upon in the rental agreement' (Idaho Code § 55-305(2)(a)) with no notice route. Its exceptions (an oral agreement, or 'a written thirty (30) day notice of the change' (Idaho Code § 55-305(2)(b)(ii))) attach only to a charge 'That is not included in the rental agreement' (Idaho Code § 55-305(2)(b)). This is the cautious reading of the section's structure, and it matches the existing `edu-fees-in-lease-id`. Every row now states it this way (§5).

**Idaho item 7: the three central shared edits (one line each).**
- `smoking-policy`: confirmed. The Clean Indoor Air Act (Title 39, Chapter 55) reaches public places only, and Idaho Code § 39-5511 lets local law go further; no Idaho statute limits a smoking ban in a lease. Its cost sentence is item 6 (proposed `smoking-policy-id`, §6).
- `no-alterations`: confirmed. Its last sentence preserves the reasonable-modification rule, which is Idaho Code § 67-5909(8)(h); the prompt's pointer "(8)(a)" is the refusal-to-transact rule. No Idaho statute gives a tenant a right to install a dish, antenna, solar device or EV charger (`edu-telecom-access-id`, `edu-no-portable-solar-right-id`, `edu-no-ev-charging-right-id`). Idaho Code § 55-3208 binds homeowner's associations only. The federal over-the-air reception rule (FEDERAL: 47 C.F.R. § 1.4000) is preserved by the same last sentence.
- `addendum-precedence`: confirmed. The federal lead disclosure (FEDERAL: 42 U.S.C. § 4852d) and any notice Idaho law requires control through the clause's own exception; Idaho's meth law imposes cleanup and vacancy duties, not tenant notice text (`edu-meth-cleanup-id`).

**Idaho item 6: charges without a stated amount** (Idaho Code § 55-305(2)(a): 'In an amount greater than that agreed upon in the rental agreement'). Whether a cost formula is an amount "agreed upon" is case law, not read, so the cautious course is a stated objective measure: proposed `keys-id`, `smoking-policy-id`, `hoa-compliance-id` (HOA fines passed through at the amount imposed, no markup; Idaho Code § 55-3206) and `pet-policy-id` (entry only under the lease's entry clause; no seizure; animal-control report, Idaho Code § 25-3511; after the independent check, pet damage, waste and odor are charged at the contractor's bill or the landlord's cost of materials, with no markup); `parking-vehicle-rules-id` is a shared edit with IA and NM (rule 62). All in §6.

**Idaho item 8.**
- (a) `early-termination-ks`: its fee (the greater of one month's rent or 30% of the remaining rent) rests on the penalty doctrine, case law not read; Idaho has no liquidated-damages statute (Battery H1-B16) and no mitigation statute (Battery H1-B25). Proposed `early-termination-id`, flat one month (§6).
- (b) `holdover-ca`: confirmed against Idaho Code § 6-303(1), Idaho Code § 6-316(1) and Idaho Code § 6-317 (treble damages discretionary). No `holdover-rate` clause is offered; see that topic's row.
- (c) `casualty-termination-id`: first sentence proposed with the `tenant-caused-damage-id` fault test (§6).
- (d) CARES: none of `edu-nonpayment-notice-id`, `edu-cure-notice-id`, `edu-eviction-process-id`, `periodic-tenancy-notice-id`, `edu-nonrenewal-notice-id` or `edu-expedited-drug-eviction-id` contradicts `edu-cares-act-notice`.

**Idaho item 9: arbitration. Proposal: tag `arbitration-agreement` ID.** Both of Taylor's conditions are met on statute, not only case law. (1) Allowed: 'a provision in a written contract to submit to arbitration any controversy thereafter arising between the parties is valid, enforceable and irrevocable' (Idaho Code § 7-901), excluding only employer-employee agreements; arbitration is saved from the bar on restricting suit 'so long as the contract does not require arbitration to be conducted outside the state of Idaho' (Idaho Code § 29-110(1)). The clause's carve-outs fit: possession stays in court (Idaho Code § 6-310, Idaho Code § 6-311A), small claims to $15,000 stay in the small claims department (Idaho Code § 1-2301), deposit claims stay in court. (2) No penalty: Idaho has no list of prohibited residential lease terms (Battery H1-B1) and no penalty for including or enforcing one (Battery H1-B20); Idaho Code § 6-317, Idaho Code § 6-320 and Idaho Code § 6-321 attach to conduct, and the Consumer Protection Act's private remedy (Idaho Code § 48-608) names no lease term. Proposed ID segment, validator-clean: "ID: Applies as written. Idaho Code § 7-901 (Uniform Arbitration Act) makes 'a provision in a written contract to submit to arbitration any controversy thereafter arising between the parties' (Idaho Code § 7-901) 'valid, enforceable and irrevocable' (Idaho Code § 7-901) save on contract grounds, excluding only employer-employee agreements; no consumer, lease or tort exclusion. Idaho Code § 29-110(1) voids terms restricting suit in Idaho tribunals or shortening the time to sue but saves arbitration 'so long as the contract does not require arbitration to be conducted outside the state of Idaho' (Idaho Code § 29-110(1)); (d) holds it in the county where the Premises are and (c) shortens no time limit. (c) keeps the right to a lawyer, which can't be waived in advance (Idaho Code § 7-906). (b)(1) keeps possession in court, where the expedited track is tried by the court without a jury (Idaho Code § 6-310, Idaho Code § 6-311A); (b)(2) keeps small claims (to $15,000, Idaho Code § 1-2301) there; (b)(3) keeps deposit claims (Idaho Code § 6-320(a)(4), Idaho Code § 6-321) in court. No penalty attaches to including or enforcing a prohibited lease term (Battery H1-B1, Battery H1-B20; edu-knowing-use-penalty-id). section-open (corpus 2026-11-09)." The tag is not in the delta; `edu-binding-arbitration-id` describes the clause's fit without saying it is offered.

**Other decisions.**
- `informal-dispute-resolution-or` not tagged: a required pre-suit notice-and-meeting step may be a term restricting a party 'from enforcing his rights under the contract in Idaho tribunals' (Idaho Code § 29-110(1)), which is case law; `edu-informal-dispute-resolution-id` explains the voluntary route.
- Open-ended mid-term rent increase (H2's question): not offered. Rule 54 education row `edu-rent-escalation-id` records that it is lawful if reserved, with 30 days' written notice (Idaho Code § 55-304(2)); `rent-escalation-id` offers a fixed schedule or index instead (one clause per topic, rule 57).
- Cost-passing clauses (H2 class decision): every new Idaho clause that passes a cost to the tenant states the landlord's dollar maximum and gives 30 days' written notice with the bill (Idaho Code § 55-305(2)(a)).
- Manufactured-home communities (Title 55, Chapter 20) and floating-home moorages (Title 55, Chapter 27) have their own rules; rows note them only where a topic touches them (ID log §7 deprioritized them).
- Crime-free clause (H3's question): the 2026-09-30 decision stands; no crime-free or drug-free clause is offered for Idaho. Drug activity is a statutory ground (Idaho Code § 6-303(5)); violence and threats reach eviction through covenants the Idaho lease already has (`residential-use-only`, `no-disturbance`, enforced by `default-by-tenant` with the notice Idaho Code § 6-303(3) requires). `edu-criminal-activity-id` is the rule 54 education row; `criminal-activity-ky` is not tagged.
- Owner move-in: the tag of `owner-occupancy-termination-ar` stands, with its note that whether an early end under a reserved right is "the expiration of the term" for a holdover eviction (Idaho Code § 6-303(1)) is case law, so a three-day notice is the cautious course.
- Business improvement district disclosure (H4): `special-district-representation-id` is offered as a conditional clause (statutory form, Idaho Code § 50-2624), never a default. Builder note for Claude Code (product work, not a row): the clause needs a yes/no question, "Is the property inside a city business improvement district?". Not put to Taylor: it changes no legal answer.
- Entity landlords in court: no Idaho statute or court rule says whether an LLC or corporation may bring an eviction through a non-lawyer. The row gives the cautious course: a lawyer for evictions; an owner or employee in small claims (Idaho Code § 1-2307(2)).
- Tenant repair agreements (H6's question): adopted. The 2026-09-30 pass declined the clause because Idaho has no statute on it, but under rule 54 a lawful term with no statute must be offered or explained. `tenant-repair-agreement-id` is optional (CONDITIONAL, not a default). It keeps the landlord's Idaho Code § 6-320(a)(1)-(3) duties with the landlord, bars tenant plumbing, HVAC, gas and electrical work (licensing exemptions cover only owners: Idaho Code § 54-2602(1)(a), Idaho Code § 54-5002(1)(a), Idaho Code § 54-1016(2)(a)), bars hiring contractors without consent (Idaho Code § 45-501), and says parts belong to the landlord. `designated-repairer-id`, `heat-freeze-protection-id` and `pest-cooperation-id` are optional on the same footing.
- Pest treatment cost: `pest-cooperation-pr` and `pest-cooperation-la` are not tagged, because they charge "the reasonable cost of treatment" with no stated amount (Idaho Code § 55-305(2)(a); item 6 class decision). `pest-cooperation-id` charges the third-party bill with no markup.
- Sex-offender occupant clause (H5): declined. It is lawful, but Idaho gives the landlord no safe harbor for it, Idaho Code § 18-8325(1) removes any duty to inquire, and the residence limits bind the registrant; `edu-sex-offender-occupancy-id` is the rule 54 row.
- Fire and building codes: the State Fire Marshal's rule (IDAPA 18.08.01) does not name the edition of the International Fire Code in force; only its cover summary mentions the 2000 edition and later adopted editions. `edu-fire-code-standard-id` tells the landlord to ask the fire marshal or local fire department. The fire code's existing-building requirements apply only where the fire code official finds a distinct hazard (IDAPA 18.08.01.030), so there is no general sprinkler retrofit duty.
- Utilities (H7):
  - `utility-transfer-id` is offered instead of tagging `utility-transfer-nj`, which bars the landlord from closing its own account; Idaho allows that. The Idaho clause allows it after at least 10 days' written notice and bars any shutoff used to force a move-out.
  - `no-liens-id` is offered instead of `no-liens-ar`, whose "Landlord's reasonable costs" sentence has no stated amount (Idaho Code § 55-305(2)).
  - `utility-billing-wv` is not tagged, because its no-fee sentence is a limit Idaho law does not impose. `utility-billing-id` caps billing at the provider's actual charges as a named drafting judgment: whether billing above cost makes a landlord a regulated utility (Idaho Code § 61-117, Idaho Code § 61-119) is unsettled.
- Automatic renewal: Idaho Code § 48-603G (online automatic renewals) may reach a lease signed online, since "goods" includes real property (Idaho Code § 48-602(6)). Cautious course: `automatic-renewal-id` (optional) puts the renewal terms in conspicuous type, offers an online way to cancel, and sends a reminder 30 to 60 days ahead for renewal terms of 12 months or more.
- Spouses: Idaho Code § 32-912 (community real property) and Idaho Code § 55-1007 (homestead) may require both spouses to join in a lease; neither says whether a lease counts. This is noted in `edu-lease-term-limits-id`. Builder note for Claude Code (product work): ask whether the property is community property or a homestead.
- Use, privacy and screening (H8): three lawful options are declined, each with a rule 54 education row.
  - A lease ban on keeping firearms at home (`edu-firearms-id`): the reach of Idaho Const. art. I, § 11 to a private lease is unsettled. `firearm-rules-ks` (tagged) covers discharge, storage and damage.
  - A mandatory portal for notices and signatures (`edu-tenant-portal-id`): Idaho Code § 28-50-105(c) gives a right, which can't be waived, to refuse further electronic transactions.
  - A barred-person covenant (`edu-trespass-notice-id`): `barred-person-covenant-ne` rests on Nebraska certification terms. An Idaho version on Idaho Code § 18-7008 is possible if wanted.
- `animal-care-access-vi` (tagged) fits the proposed `pet-policy-id`: the animal clause is the tenant's own consent to let a named person in, and its fallback goes to law enforcement or animal care and control, the officers Idaho Code § 25-3511 empowers. `pet-policy-id` gives the landlord no seizure right.
- `edu-optional-lease-terms-id` now lists every optional Idaho clause and every optional shared clause tagged ID in this pass. `special-district-representation-id` is listed as required where it applies. If the arbitration tag proposed above is accepted, add "binding arbitration of money claims (arbitration-agreement)" to that list at sync.

  - `jury-waiver` (I.R.C.P. 38, I.R.C.P. 39);
  - `confession-of-judgment` and `entity-landlord-court-representation` (I.R.C.P. search);
  - `utility-deposit-return` (IDAPA 31.21.01);
  - `subsidy-late-fee` (24 C.F.R. part 982);
  - `health-district-rental-rules` (IDAPA index; H4 updated its own row);
  - `tenant-bankruptcy` (11 U.S.C. § 362, § 365(d)(1));
  - `eviction-service-party`, `rent-into-court-counterclaim` and `minor-tenants` (I.R.C.P. 4, I.R.C.P. 13, I.R.C.P. 17);

  - `subsidy-habitability-proration` and `subsidized-inspection-refusal` (24 C.F.R. § 982.404, § 982.405, § 982.453, § 982.551);
  - `telecom-access` (47 C.F.R. § 1.4000, excerpt).
- Not fetched (optional; the rows stand without them): 25 U.S.C. § 415 and 25 C.F.R. part 162 (`communal-land-lease-approval`); 15 U.S.C. § 1681s-2 (`rent-reporting`); IDAPA 58.01.08, public drinking water systems (`private-well-testing`; reaches only a landlord whose own system meets Idaho Code § 39-103(12)); FEDERAL: 42 U.S.C. § 3602, § 3603(b), § 3607 and the FCRA sections 15 U.S.C. § 1681b, § 1681m, § 1681w with 16 C.F.R. § 682.3 (b8 screening, records and children-occupancy rows, which name them as not reviewed).

**Helper decisions and questions** (as filed by the canvass helpers; the main pass adopted each unless noted above):

- [H1, decision, `liquidated-damages`] For the main pass, re early-termination-ks (Idaho tag; fee 'one month's Rent ... or 30% of the remaining Rent due under the Term, whichever is greater'): Idaho has no liquidated-damages statute for leases of real property (Battery H1-B16) and no statutory mitigation rule (Battery H1-B25, edu-abandonment-and-mitigation-id), so the fee's enforceability turns on Idaho case law (penalty doctrine) not read, plus Idaho Code § 55-305(1) reasonableness. The greater-of formula can exceed actual loss late in a long term when the unit re-lets quickly; that is the shape most exposed to a penalty finding. Options: (a) keep the tag with a sentence in the ID segment stating the case-law risk; (b) cap the Idaho fee at the lesser of the formula and the rent for the remaining Term; (c) read Idaho case law on liquidated damages before deciding. Recommendation: (c), then (b) if the cases apply a reasonable-forecast test.
- [H1, decision, `waiver-by-acceptance`] Idaho has no statute on waiver by accepting rent (Battery H1-B21). The library offers no rent-acceptance non-waiver clause for Idaho (the only such clause found, rent-acceptance-nonwaiver-as, is single-state). If Taylor wants one offered in Idaho, it would be an optional SERVES_LANDLORD clause whose effect is case law; recommendation: do not add it until Idaho waiver cases are read, keeping the cautious course in edu-waiver-by-acceptance-id.
- [H1, decision, `abandonment-and-mitigation`] For the main pass, re early-termination-ks (Idaho tag; fee 'one month's Rent ... or 30% of the remaining Rent due under the Term, whichever is greater'): Idaho statutes say nothing about early move-out or a landlord's duty to re-let (Battery H1-B25, Battery H1-B26), so whether a landlord who collects the fee may also, or instead, claim rent for the rest of the term, and whether the fee is a penalty when the unit re-lets quickly, are Idaho case-law questions not read. The clause's own sentence that the tenant 'remains responsible for Rent and other obligations up to the termination date' limits rent to the termination date, so the fee is the whole of the tenant's early-exit liability under the option; nothing in Idaho statute conflicts with that. The only statutory constraint is Idaho Code § 55-305(1)-(3) (fee stated in the lease and reasonable). Recommendation: keep the Idaho tag, add to its ID segment that mitigation and penalty questions are case law not read and that default-by-tenant's mitigation promise applies "to the extent required by applicable law"; consider reading Idaho cases on liquidated damages and mitigation before the next Idaho review (see also the liquidated-damages decision finding).
- [H1, decision, `informal-dispute-resolution`] Whether to tag informal-dispute-resolution-or for Idaho. For: it is mutual, excludes possession actions and emergency relief, and says it 'does not delay or limit any notice, deadline or remedy'; no Idaho statute bars it and no penalty attaches. Against: Idaho Code § 29-110(1) voids 'Every stipulation or condition in a contract, by which any party thereto is restricted from enforcing his rights under the contract in Idaho tribunals', and a required notice-and-meeting before suit may be such a restriction (case law not read); if void it simply drops out, but the library avoids offering terms of doubtful effect. Recommendation: do not tag until Idaho cases on Idaho Code § 29-110(1) are read; edu-informal-dispute-resolution-id explains the voluntary route.
- [H1, decision, `binding-arbitration`] Recommend tagging the shared arbitration-agreement clause for Idaho (Taylor's rule, 2026-11-07: offer it only if Idaho law allows it AND no statute penalizes including, or suing to enforce, a prohibited lease term). (1) Allowed: Idaho Code § 7-901 makes a written pre-dispute arbitration provision 'valid, enforceable and irrevocable' save on contract-revocation grounds, with the only exclusion for employer-employee agreements; Idaho Code § 29-110(1) expressly saves arbitration held in Idaho; the clause's place (county of the Premises), its no-time-shortening sentence and its right-to-a-lawyer sentence fit Idaho Code § 29-110(1) and Idaho Code § 7-906; its carve-outs fit Idaho (possession, Idaho Code § 6-310 and Idaho Code § 6-311A; small claims, Idaho Code § 1-2301, $15,000; deposit claims). The answer rests on statute, not only case law. (2) No penalty: Idaho has no list of prohibited residential lease terms (Battery H1-B1) and no knowing-use penalty (Battery H1-B20); Idaho Code § 6-317, Idaho Code § 6-320 and Idaho Code § 6-321 attach to conduct, not lease terms; the Consumer Protection Act reaches leasing (Idaho Code § 48-602(2)) and its private remedy (Idaho Code § 48-608: voidable agreement, actual damages or $1,000, fees) could reach an unconscionable or deceptive practice, but no listed practice names a lease term, and the clause's mutuality, opt-out and landlord-paid fees answer Idaho Code § 48-603C. Residual doubt: the jury right (Idaho Const. art. I, § 7, waiver 'in such manner as may be prescribed by law') - the Uniform Arbitration Act is such a law, and the shared clause's notes on Kindred Nursing Centers v. Clark apply; Idaho cases not read. Proposed ID segment (validator-clean draft): ID: Applies as written. Idaho Code § 7-901 (Uniform Arbitration Act) makes 'a provision in a written contract to submit to arbitration any controversy thereafter arising between the parties' (Idaho Code § 7-901) 'valid, enforceable and irrevocable' (Idaho Code § 7-901) save on contract grounds, excluding only employer-employee agreements; no consumer, lease or tort exclusion. Idaho Code § 29-110(1) voids terms restricting suit in Idaho tribunals or shortening the time to sue but saves arbitration 'so long as the contract does not require arbitration to be conducted outside the state of Idaho' (Idaho Code § 29-110(1)); (d) holds it in the county where the Premises are and (c) shortens no time limit. (c) keeps the right to a lawyer, which can't be waived in advance (Idaho Code § 7-906). (b)(1) keeps possession in court, where the expedited track is tried by the court without a jury (Idaho Code § 6-310, Idaho Code § 6-311A); (b)(2) keeps small claims (to $15,000, Idaho Code § 1-2301) there; (b)(3) keeps deposit claims (Idaho Code § 6-320(a)(4), Idaho Code § 6-321) in court; claims for specific performance under Idaho Code § 6-320 are not money claims and stay in court. No penalty attaches to including or enforcing a prohibited lease term (Battery H1-B1, Battery H1-B20; edu-knowing-use-penalty-id). section-open (corpus 2026-11-09).
- [H2, decision, `government-fee-reimbursement`] Shared clause government-fee-reimbursement-ky not tagged for ID: its reimbursement amount is open-ended ('[all / the following share of] the fee') and its payment period is a free bracket, so it may charge an amount not 'agreed upon in the rental agreement' (Idaho Code § 55-305(2)(a)). Idaho's own row government-fee-reimbursement-id names each fee, caps it at a stated dollar maximum and gives 30 days' written notice with the bill.
- [H2, decision, `governmental-fines`] Shared clauses tenant-caused-fines-as and tenant-caused-fines-oh not tagged for ID: the -as clause has no dollar limit and a free notice bracket, so the reimbursed fine may not be an amount 'agreed upon in the rental agreement' (Idaho Code § 55-305(2)(a)); the -oh clause rests on Ohio's R.C. 5321.13(D). Idaho's own row governmental-fines-id adds a stated maximum and 30 days' written notice.
- [H2, decision, `alarm-tampering-fee`] Shared clause detector-care-la not tagged for ID: its no-disable and reporting duties duplicate the ID clause smoke-detectors-id (alarm-duties), and its 'reasonable cost' charge is open-ended under Idaho Code § 55-305(2)(a). Education row edu-alarm-tampering-fee-id records that a fixed tampering fee is lawful if stated and reasonable but is not offered (rule 54).
- [H2, decision, `carpet-cleaning`] Shared clause carpet-cleaning-ar not tagged for ID: it charges 'the reasonable cost Landlord actually pays' with no stated maximum (Idaho Code § 55-305(2)(a) bars a cost 'In an amount greater than that agreed upon in the rental agreement') and does not exclude cleaning needed only from normal wear and tear (Idaho Code § 6-321(1)). Idaho's own row carpet-cleaning-id adds both.
- [H2, question, `rent-escalation`] Should the Idaho library offer an open-ended mid-term rent increase clause (the rent-increase-midterm-ky shape: 30 days' written notice, tenant may exit) without its non-retaliation sentence? Idaho law allows it if the lease reserves it, with the Idaho Code § 55-304(2) notice. Recommendation: no for now; offer rent-escalation-id (fixed schedule or index), and revisit if landlords ask. edu-rent-escalation-id records the decline under rule 54.
- [H2, decision, `government-fee-reimbursement`] Class decision (b2): every new Idaho clause that passes a cost to the tenant (government-fee-reimbursement-id, governmental-fines-id, carpet-cleaning-id, tax-escalation-id) states the landlord's own dollar maximum and gives 30 days' written notice with the bill. Reason: Idaho Code § 55-305(2)(a) bars a charge 'In an amount greater than that agreed upon in the rental agreement', and whether an uncapped 'actual cost' is an agreed amount is case law not read; a stated maximum makes the amount agreed under every reading. Each such clause also says the amount is not Rent, so it stays out of an Idaho Code § 6-303(2) nonpayment notice.
- [H3, question, `criminal-activity`] Does the 2026-09-30 decision to offer no crime-free clause for Idaho still hold? It holds for a typical crime-free addendum that promises termination for one violation without cure (Idaho Code § 6-303(3) may require a three-day notice to perform, and whether a crime "cannot afterward be performed" is unread case law). The recorded reason ("the drug ground is statutory", Idaho Code § 6-303(5)) covers drugs only; violence and threats reach eviction only through a lease covenant. But the Idaho lease already carries that covenant (residential-use-only: no "illegal" use; no-disturbance: no condition threatening persons or property) and default-by-tenant enforces it with the notice the law requires. The shared clause criminal-activity-ky (13 states) does not overpromise: it terminates "with the notice that this Lease's Default by Tenant terms and applicable law require" and carves out victims and calls for help. Recommendation: keep the decision (no separate clause); the existing covenants already do the work. If Taylor prefers an express clause, criminal-activity-ky could be tagged for ID as written, and edu-criminal-activity-id would then need its "not offered" sentences changed.
- [H3, decision, `drug-free-housing-addendum`] Shared clause drug-free-housing-ca (CA, SC, UT) not tagged for ID: its cannabis and accommodation sentences rest on California law, and the standing ID decision offers no crime-free or drug-free clause because Idaho Code § 6-303(5) makes drug activity a ground without any lease term. Rule 54 education row edu-no-drug-free-addendum-id written.
- [H3, decision, `dv-lockchange`] No lock-change clause written for Idaho: no statute creates a right, duty or cost rule, and the keys clause (tagged ID) already covers re-keying charges; edu-no-dv-lockchange-id gives the protection-order course. Battery H3-B3 confirms edu-no-dv-termination-id on lock changes; Battery H3-B1 confirms it on termination and eviction protection; Battery H3-B4 confirms it on calls for help.
- [H3, decision, `owner-move-in-reservation`] Tagged owner-occupancy-termination-ar for ID rather than writing an Idaho clause: no Idaho owner move-in statute (Battery H3-B16), no just-cause rule, clause charges nothing and ends with possession only through a court eviction. No separate education row written; the tag note records the open question whether an early end under a reserved right is "the expiration of the term" under Idaho Code § 6-303(1).
- [H3, decision, `cold-weather-vacate-notice`] Tagged cold-weather-vacate-notice-wy for ID (generic, RECOMMENDED, SERVES_LANDLORD). No Idaho cold-weather, absence-notice or minimum-heat statute (Battery H3-B15). Its heating carve-out preserves Idaho Code § 6-320(a)(2). Its freeze-damage sentence is a damages term, read like tenant-caused-damage-id under Idaho Code § 55-305.
- [H4, decision, `special-district-representation`] New Idaho clause special-district-representation-id (LEASE_CLAUSE, CONDITIONAL/REQUIRED, not a default): business improvement district disclosure in the statutory form of Idaho Code § 50-2624(2), owner-signed, used only for property inside a BID. No new variable; signatures and dates are bracketed prompts. The builder needs a yes/no question ("Is the property inside a city business improvement district?") to include it.
- [H5, decision, `sex-offender-occupancy`] Optional clause declined (rule 54): a tenant statement or covenant that no occupant will reside in violation of Idaho Code § 18-8331 (max two registrants per unit) or Idaho Code § 18-8329(1)(d) (500 feet from school/daycare) is lawful under the statutes read, but Idaho gives the landlord no safe harbor for it, Idaho Code § 18-8325(1) removes any duty to inquire, and the duty already falls on the registrant; federal fair housing limits on criminal-record screening were not reviewed. edu-sex-offender-occupancy-id says so. Recommendation: keep it declined unless Taylor wants parity with the optional Birmingham-only statement offered in Alabama.
- [H5, question, `automatic-renewal`] Possible reach of Idaho's online automatic-renewal law to leases (not a b5 topic; for whoever owns automatic-renewal). 'Automatic subscription renewal' means 'an agreement entered into via the internet to provide goods or services to an Idaho consumer for a specified time and price that is automatically renewed at the end of a definite term for a subsequent term unless the consumer cancels the agreement' (Idaho Code § 48-603G(1)(a)); a 'Seller' includes 'a person who sells, leases, or offers to sell or lease automatic subscription renewals' (Idaho Code § 48-603G(1)(c)); and the act defines '"Goods" mean any property, tangible or intangible, real, personal or mixed' (Idaho Code § 48-602(6)). A residential lease signed online with an automatic renewal term might therefore need the 'clearly and conspicuously' disclosure of renewal terms and cancellation methods (Idaho Code § 48-603G(2)), an easy online cancellation method (Idaho Code § 48-603G(3)), and, for renewals of twelve months or more, notice 30-60 days ahead (Idaho Code § 48-603G(4)). No private right of action (Idaho Code § 48-603G(5)); applies to agreements entered into or renewed on and after January 1, 2023 (Idaho Code § 48-603G(7)). Whether a lease is 'goods or services' here is case law, not read. Recommendation: the Idaho automatic-renewal row should state the risk and the cautious course (disclose the renewal term and how to cancel conspicuously in any online-signed lease).
- [H6, decision, `tenant-repair-agreement`] The 2026-09-30 pass declined an Idaho tenant-repair-agreement clause because Idaho has no statutory framework. Under rule 54 that reason does not hold: with no statute the agreement is a lawful contract term, and a lawful option must be offered or explained. H6 wrote tenant-repair-agreement-id (optional clause) and edu-tenant-repair-agreement-id. Idaho-specific limits built in: keeps the Idaho Code § 6-320(a)(1)-(3) duties with the landlord (waiver is case law); bars tenant plumbing, HVAC, gas and electrical work because the licensing exemptions cover only a person who 'owns or is a contract purchaser of the premises' (Idaho Code § 54-2602(1)(a), Idaho Code § 54-5002(1)(a)) or an owner's own residence (Idaho Code § 54-1016(2)(a)); bars hiring contractors without consent (mechanics' lien, Idaho Code § 45-501); parts belong to the landlord. Not tagged: tenant-repair-agreement-hi and maintenance-allocation-ks rest on Uniform-Act and Kansas statutes. Recommendation: adopt; Taylor may prefer to keep the 2026-09-30 decline, in which case keep only the education row and change its last paragraph.
- [H6, decision, `bed-bug-cooperation`] Shared pest-cooperation-pr (12 states) and pest-cooperation-la not tagged for Idaho: each lets the landlord charge "the reasonable cost of treatment", an amount the lease does not state; under the main-pass class decision on Idaho Code § 55-305(2) (Idaho item 6: stated objective method) H6 wrote pest-cooperation-id, the same text with a third-party-bill, no-markup charge and the repairs-belong-to-landlord sentence. If the main pass later decides a reasonable-cost formula meets Idaho Code § 55-305(2)(a), pest-cooperation-id can be dropped and pest-cooperation-pr tagged instead.
- [H7, decision, `utility-transfer`] Shared clause utility-transfer-nj (NJ;SC;AL;UT) was not tagged for Idaho: its last sentence ("Landlord will not shut off, interrupt or ask a provider to discontinue any utility service to the Premises because Tenant has not transferred an account") gives away an option Idaho law leaves to the landlord, since no statute bars a customer from ending its own account (Battery H7-B7) and the PUC rules let the utility end service at the customer's request with two business days' notice to occupants (IDAPA 31.21.01.311, IDAPA 31.21.01.312). Idaho's own optional clause utility-transfer-id keeps the account-closing option after at least 10 days' written notice and bars any other interruption or a closure meant to force a move-out. Taylor may prefer the NJ wording (no cutoff at all) as the more cautious course; if so, tag utility-transfer-nj instead and retire utility-transfer-id.
- [H7, decision, `construction-liens`] Shared clause no-liens-ar (16 states) was not tagged for Idaho because its sentence "will pay Landlord's reasonable costs caused by it" charges an amount the lease does not state; under the main pass's Idaho Code § 55-305(2) cautious course (stated amount or objective method) Idaho gets its own no-liens-id, identical except that the cost sentence is dropped (the landlord keeps its damages claim). If Taylor treats breach damages as outside Idaho Code § 55-305(2), tag no-liens-ar instead and retire no-liens-id.
- [H7, decision, `utility-apportionment`] Shared clause utility-billing-wv (WV;AL) was not tagged for Idaho: its sentence "Landlord will not add any administrative or service fee to these charges" is a limit Idaho law does not impose (tag rule). Idaho gets its own optional clause utility-billing-id, which keeps a cap at the provider's actual charges and no billing fee as a named drafting judgment (utility status under Idaho Code § 61-119(1) and Idaho Code § 61-117, 'not for sale to others', is for PUC orders, not read).
- [H7, decision, `alterations`] Item 7 (no-alterations): no Idaho statute found gives a tenant a right to install a satellite dish, antenna, cable, solar device or EV charger, or limits a landlord's refusal (Battery H7-B15, Battery H7-B16, Battery H7-B17). Idaho Code § 55-3208 binds homeowner's associations as to owners' rooftops only, and Idaho Code § 50-3008(2) lets video providers decline buildings they may not enter. Nothing conflicts with no-alterations. The one outside source left open is the federal over-the-air reception devices rule (47 C.F.R. § 1.4000; fetch filed), which no-alterations already preserves through its last sentence. The fixtures sentence in no-alterations' Idaho note (Idaho Code § 55-307) was re-read and stands.
- [H7, decision, `optional-lease-terms`] edu-optional-lease-terms-id lists only optional clauses already in the library or added/tagged by this batch. When the main pass merges the other helpers' Idaho optional clauses (from this pass: government-fee-reimbursement-id, governmental-fines-id, carpet-cleaning-id, rent-escalation-id, tax-escalation-id, special-district-representation-id, tenant-repair-agreement-id, designated-repairer-id, heat-freeze-protection-id, pest-cooperation-id, and ID tags such as showing-notice-la, lease-copy-receipt-hi, move-in-condition-report-as, rent-concession-wi, rent-installments-or, owner-occupancy-termination-ar, cold-weather-vacate-notice-wy, rental-inspection-cooperation-mn, eminent-domain-vi, association-documents-delivery-ks, energy-data-release-wy, lock-change-ak, appliances-excluded-la, furnishings-included-la), add a sentence naming those accepted, or replace the inventory with a generated list.
- [H7, question, `automatic-renewal`] Whether Idaho Code § 48-603G reaches an automatically renewing residential lease signed online is unsettled: it covers 'an agreement entered into via the internet to provide goods or services' (Idaho Code § 48-603G(1)(a)) and goods include real property (Idaho Code § 48-602(6)), but no case law or Attorney General rule was read. Recommendation: keep the cautious course in automatic-renewal-id (disclose terms and cancellation methods conspicuously, an online cancel method, a 30-60 day reminder for renewal terms of 12 months or more) rather than drop the clause; the section bars a private suit and civil penalties against good-faith compliers (Idaho Code § 48-603G(5), (6)), so the cost of compliance is low.
- [H7, question, `lease-term-limitation`] edu-lease-term-limits-id recommends both spouses sign a lease of community property or a homestead, and sign before a notary for leases over one year, because Idaho Code § 32-912 and Idaho Code § 55-1007 bar one spouse alone from conveying or encumbering community real estate or the homestead and neither says whether a lease counts; Idaho Code § 55-1007(1) shows consent by 'Each spouse executing and acknowledging the instrument'. The notary step is a drafting judgment. Taylor may want the lease builder to ask whether the landlord is married and the property is community property or a homestead.
- [H8, decision, `tenant-screening`] Idaho Code § 18-8331 (a registered sex offender may not reside in a residential dwelling unit with more than one other registrant; no one may operate a residence house in violation; misdemeanor for a knowing and intentional violation) bears on the library topic sex-offender-occupancy, which this batch did not cover. edu-tenant-screening-id mentions it; whoever answers sex-offender-occupancy for Idaho should cite it (read whole, chapter PDF).
- [H8, question, `trespass-notice`] Taylor decision: Idaho has no barred-person lease covenant. barred-person-covenant-ne was not tagged because its domestic-violence carve-out uses Nebraska certification terms and would add a limit Idaho law does not impose. Recommendation: if Taylor wants one, write an Idaho variant (barred-person-covenant-id) on Idaho Code § 18-7008(2)(a), (5)-(7), keeping an emergency/law-enforcement exception and replacing the Nebraska carve-out with a plain exception for a person the tenant is required by law or court order to admit; until then edu-trespass-notice-id says the covenant is lawful but not offered.
- [H8, question, `firearms`] Taylor decision: edu-firearms-id says a lease ban on keeping firearms in the home is not barred by any Idaho statute but is not offered (Idaho Const. art. I, § 11 reach to private leases unsettled; Idaho Code § 18-3302(25) preserves private owner and tenant rights). Recommendation: keep it unoffered; firearm-rules-ks (tagged ID) covers discharge, storage and damage.
- [H8, question, `tenant-portal`] Taylor decision: no Idaho clause makes the portal mandatory for notices and signatures, because Idaho Code § 28-50-105(c) gives a non-waivable right to refuse further electronic transactions. Recommendation: keep declining; a payments-only portal term already fits acceptable-payment-methods (tagged ID).

### §5 Independent check
Two rounds, one checker at a time, each briefed only from the delivered rows and the primary sources (corpus, `fetched/`, battery records), never from the helper briefs. Every finding quotes its source and gives exact old and new text. All accepted changes were applied once and recorded in `errata.jsonl` (194 entries).

**Round 1** checked all 246 new rows, the 26 tag segments and the 5 proposed override rows, in four parts, each passing its error shapes to the next.

| Part | Rows | Errors | Minor |
|---|---|---|---|
| A1 | 63 | 8 | 20 |
| A2 | 64 | 18 | 20 |
| B1 | 72 | 5 | 34 |
| B2 | 78 | 10 | 27 |

All 142 findings were accepted. Main-pass sweeps of the same shapes across all parts added:
- 7 fixes for the § 55-305(2) structure;
- the "FEDERAL:" prefix on 26 federal citations in 21 rows;
- 4 quote and citation repairs.

Recurring shapes:
- **The fee statute read wrongly.** The 30-day-notice exception in Idaho Code § 55-305(2)(b) covers only a charge the agreement does not include. A charge above the agreed amount, § 55-305(2)(a), has no notice route. Some rows also dropped the oral-agreement and notice routes altogether. The helper brief's own summary had the first slip.
- **Dropped scope qualifiers:**
  - the five-acre agricultural exclusion of the tenant's action (Idaho Code § 6-320(e));
  - the limits of the fast track and judge-only trial (Idaho Code § 6-310(1), § 6-311A);
  - the fallback-only methods of notice service (Idaho Code § 6-304, § 6-323(2));
  - federal "during the term" limits.
- **Exceptions inside the cited unit dropped**, e.g. Idaho Code § 6-324, § 18-7008(5), § 67-6541(6), § 55-303, § 29-110(1) (arbitration).
- **"Lawful" stated where the sources show only that no statute bars a term.**
- **Absence claims that missed a statute:**
  - the floating-home waiver ban (Idaho Code § 55-2706(5));
  - floating-home utility billing (Idaho Code § 55-2708);
  - the business improvement district lease form (Idaho Code § 50-2624(2));
  - the servicemember lease-termination right (FEDERAL: 50 U.S.C. § 3955).

The underlying batteries passed; their patterns were too narrow for those wordings. Two other battery hits were misdescribed in notes.

**Round 2** re-checked the 125 rows and tag segments edited after round 1: 1 error and 16 minor findings, all accepted. The error was a carbon monoxide statement made too broad by a round-1 edit. The minor findings covered the oral-agreement route, citations doubled by an edit, two pinpoints, one wrong "not reviewed" label, and one unprefixed federal citation. Every "FEDERAL:" prefix was confirmed in place.

### §6 Findings on existing rows
Proposed changes to existing rows, each with the statute quoted and the wording proposed. Claude Code applies them at sync; nothing here is in the delta.


- [H3, `dv-protection-order-chapter-moved`] edu-no-dv-termination-id is confirmed by Batteries H3-B1, H3-B3 and H3-B4, but its note says "VAWA (34 U.S.C. § 12491) not read (rule 21)"; the section is now saved and read (fetched/FED-34USC12491.txt). Its body ("Federal law (the Violence Against Women Act) protects tenants in covered federally assisted housing") is consistent with FEDERAL: 34 U.S.C. § 12491(b)(1). Suggest the main pass update the note to cite the read text and point to edu-no-dv-eviction-protection-id. Its note also uses the "§§" form, which the current validator rejects for new rows.
- [H3, `statutory-early-termination`] edu-consumer-protection-act-id says "The Act's remedies and the Attorney General's rules were not read". Idaho Code § 48-608 is now read whole (chapter PDF): a person who leases goods (which include real property, Idaho Code § 48-602(6)) and suffers an ascertainable loss from an unlawful practice 'may treat any agreement incident thereto as voidable or, in the alternative, may bring an action to recover actual damages or one thousand dollars ($1,000), whichever is the greater' (Idaho Code § 48-608(1)), with attorney fees to a prevailing plaintiff (Idaho Code § 48-608(5)) and an enhanced remedy for elderly or disabled persons (Idaho Code § 48-608(2)). Suggest the main pass add the remedy to that row's note or body; edu-statutory-early-termination-id and edu-no-double-letting-rule-id now rely on it.
- [H3, `statutory-early-termination`] edu-no-servicemember-statute-id says "Federal SCRA (50 U.S.C. § 3955) not read (rule 21)". The section is now saved and was read whole (fetched/FED-50USC3955.txt). Its body is consistent with it. Suggest updating the note to cite FEDERAL: 50 U.S.C. § 3955(a)(1), (c), (d)(1)(A), (e)(1), (f), and pointing to edu-statutory-early-termination-id, which states the monthly-rent effective date (30 days after the next rent due date), the bar on an early termination charge and the 30-day refund of prepaid rent.
- [H5, `foreclosure-disclosure`] edu-foreclosure-tenants-id cites Idaho Code § 45-1506(11) as 'read in context'. It has now been read whole (corpus 2026-11-09, section page); the row's statement holds: 'The purchaser at the trustee’s sale shall be entitled to possession of the property on the tenth day following the sale, and any persons remaining in possession thereafter under any interest except one prior to the deed of trust shall be deemed to be tenants at sufferance' (Idaho Code § 45-1506(11)). Its note can drop 'read in context'. Not stated by any ID row: on or after the tenth day, if the property is reasonably determined by the purchaser to be unoccupied, the purchaser may dispose of titled personal property 'in the manner described by applicable law' and may dispose of nontitled personal property 'only after providing ninety (90) days’ written notice' by first class mail and posting (Idaho Code § 45-1506(15)(a), (b)). Proposed: add one sentence to edu-foreclosure-tenants-id (main pass decision; it concerns the buyer, not the landlord). Also, the row's 'without a notice' for the tenant-at-sufferance track rests on Idaho Code § 6-310(1)(d) and Idaho Code § 6-311, which this helper did not re-read.
- [H5, `sex-offender-disclosure`] edu-psychologically-impacted-id says the stigma statute 'is written for sales; whether it protects a landlord's nondisclosure to a tenant is not settled.' For the sex-offender limb that doubt is resolved by a separate statute not cited there: 'No person or governmental entity, other than those specifically charged in this chapter with a duty to collect information under this chapter regarding registered sexual offenders, has a duty to inquire, investigate or disclose any information regarding registered sexual offenders' (Idaho Code § 18-8325(1)) and no such person 'shall be held liable for any failure to disclose any information regarding registered sexual offenders to any other person or entity' (Idaho Code § 18-8325(2)). Proposed: add to that row's body 'For a sex offender living in or near the property, a separate law says you have no duty to disclose and no liability for not disclosing (Idaho Code § 18-8325); see edu-sex-offender-disclosure-id.' The row's doubt stands for the disease and crime-site limbs.
- [H5, `association-obligations-disclosure`] edu-hoa-rental-restrictions-id says 'Condominium Property Act (Idaho Code Title 55, Chapter 15) not read for rental rules.' It has now been read for tenants: 'All condominium owners, tenants of such owners, employees of owners and tenants, or any other persons that may in any manner use property or any part thereof submitted to the provisions of this act shall be subject to this act and to the declaration and by-laws of the project adopted pursuant to the provisions of this act' (Idaho Code § 55-1516). For the main pass's proposed hoa-compliance-id override: this is the Idaho basis for binding a condominium tenant to the declaration and bylaws; for a non-condominium homeowner's association no Idaho statute binds the tenant directly (Title 55, Chapter 32 read whole; its fine and notice rules in Idaho Code § 55-3206 run to a 'member'), so the tenant's duty there rests on the lease. edu-no-association-disclosure-id states this.
- [H6, `other-landlord-facilities`] Information for edu-landlord-duties-id (no change proposed to its body): its note says the Idaho Building Code Act 'not read as rental duties'. H6 read Idaho Code Title 39, Chapter 41 whole (24 sections, corpus 2026-11-09): it authorizes code adoption and permit enforcement for construction, alteration and repair and contains no duty on a landlord of an existing rental; Idaho Code § 39-4111(3) limits permit-driven upgrades of unaffected existing parts. Separately, the Idaho Elevator Safety Code Act puts maintenance, annual certificate and QEI-inspection duties on conveyance owners (Idaho Code § 39-8609(2), Idaho Code § 39-8611, Idaho Code § 39-8617; private residences exempt, Idaho Code § 39-8606(a)(1)); now stated in edu-other-landlord-facilities-id.
- [H7, `consumer-protection-act`] edu-consumer-protection-act-id's note says 'no Idaho row has an automatic-renewal term'. This pass adds the optional clause automatic-renewal-id and edu-automatic-renewal-id, which take the cautious course under Idaho Code § 48-603G ('A seller may not make an automatic subscription renewal offer to a consumer in this state unless the seller clearly and conspicuously discloses' (Idaho Code § 48-603G(2))). Proposed: update that note sentence to point to automatic-renewal-id when these rows are accepted.
- [H8, `tenant-right-to-organize`] edu-no-retaliation-statute-id says 'Idaho has no statute barring a landlord from retaliating against a residential tenant for complaints or for exercising legal rights' and 'Federal fair housing law separately prohibits retaliation for fair housing complaints'. Idaho's own Human Rights Act also bars it for discrimination complaints: 'It shall be unlawful for a person or any business entity subject to regulation by this chapter to discriminate against any individual because he or she has opposed any practice made unlawful by this chapter or because such individual has made a charge, testified, assisted, or participated in any manner in an investigation, proceeding, or litigation under this chapter' (Idaho Code § 67-5911, read whole, chapter PDF), and the Act reaches real estate transactions including rentals (Idaho Code § 67-5909(8), Idaho Code § 67-5902(12)). Proposed: add to the body 'Idaho's Human Rights Act does bar reprisals against anyone who opposes housing discrimination or files or helps with a discrimination charge (Idaho Code § 67-5911).' The row's absence finding for general retaliation stands.
- [H8, `tenant-display-rights`] common-area-use ID note says Idaho Code § 55-3210 and Idaho Code § 55-3209 'bind homeowner's associations only'. That holds, but both protect a 'member', defined as 'any person or entity owning or possessing an interest in residential real property or a lot within the physical boundaries of an established homeowner's association' (Idaho Code § 55-3203(8), read whole, section page), so a tenant may arguably be protected against the association (not the landlord). Proposed: no change to common-area-use; edu-tenant-display-rights-id records the point.
- [MAIN, `parking-vehicle-rules`] parking-vehicle-rules-id (tagged ID, IA, NM): its sentence "may issue parking tags, decals, or access cards, the cost of which may be charged to Tenant" states no amount or method. Idaho Code § 55-305(2): 'An owner may not charge to the tenant of a rental property a fee, fine, assessment, interest, or other cost: (a) In an amount greater than that agreed upon in the rental agreement; or (b) That is not included in the rental agreement'. Whether "the cost" is an amount agreed upon is case law, not read. Cautious course, as parking-vehicle-rules-ut adopted: replace that sentence with "may issue parking tags, decals, or access cards without charge; Landlord may charge Tenant for replacing a lost or damaged tag, decal or card only the amount its supplier charges Landlord for the replacement, with no markup or added fee, and will give Tenant a copy of the supplier's bill or receipt when Landlord charges Tenant." The row is shared with IA and NM, so this is a rule 62 shared edit proposal, not an Idaho override; it only narrows what Landlord may charge, so it cannot make the clause unlawful in IA or NM, but each needs vetting at sync. Rest of the row (towing wording, Idaho Code § 49-1806(3)) unchanged.
- [MAIN, `casualty-termination`] casualty-termination-id (ID only): fault test differs from tenant-caused-damage-id ("not caused by Tenant, members of Tenant's household, or Tenant's guests" vs "the deliberate or negligent act or omission of Tenant, an Occupant, or a guest or invitee of Tenant"), and its partial-casualty abatement sentence ("If the casualty makes only part of the property unusable") does not clearly carry the fault limit (SOP rule 54, UT circle-back 2: the persons whose fault counts must be identical in both clauses). Proposed replacement of the first sentence, with "such" carried into the abatement sentence: "This Section applies only to damage or destruction of the property by fire or other casualty that does not result from the deliberate or negligent act or omission of Tenant, an Occupant, or a guest or invitee of Tenant. If such a casualty makes the property unfit to live in or substantially impairs Tenant's use of it, either Landlord or Tenant may end this Lease by written notice to the other, effective on the date Tenant moves out." and in the third sentence "If such a casualty makes only part of the property unusable ...". The rest of the row is unchanged. Idaho has no casualty statute (ID retro 2026-10-03, search of Idaho Code Titles 6, 9, 29, 41 and 55; ID log s6.1), so the fault test is a drafting judgment; Idaho Code § 6-321(1) uses a similar list for wear and tear ('the tenant or members of his household, or their invitees or guests'), which "an Occupant, or a guest or invitee of Tenant" covers.
