### §7 Findings for other states
None.


### Proposed SOP changes
1. **Rule 19 / helper brief framework: quote a statute's structure, don't paraphrase it.** The brief summarized Idaho Code § 55-305(2) so that its 30-day-notice exception appeared to cover both (a), an amount above the agreed amount, and (b), a charge not included. Helpers copied the slip into at least 12 rows. The independent check traced it to the brief. Proposed: where the brief's framework section gives a section with "unless", "except" or a list of alternatives, print the subsection text with its letters, not a summary [ID-gaps 1].
2. **Rule 80 / helper brief: a scope-qualifier list.** Most errata were dropped qualifiers on a handful of core sections: the five-acre exclusion of § 6-320(e), the fast-track scope of § 6-310(1) and § 6-311A, the fallback order of § 6-304 and § 6-323(2). Proposed: the brief lists each core section's scope qualifiers. Each body that describes the section carries the qualifier, and a script flags bodies that cite the section without it [ID-gaps 2].
3. **Rule 59 / validator: the "FEDERAL:" prefix in bodies as well as notes.** Helpers left 26 federal citations in bodies and notes unprefixed. `check.py` should flag a "U.S.C." or "C.F.R." citation without the prefix [ID-gaps 3].
4. **Rule 24 / sources: save excerpts of very long sections, marked as such.** 11 U.S.C. § 362, § 365, IDAPA 24.39.30 and IDAPA 18.08.01 were read whole in the browser, but only the parts relied on were transcribed. Each is hash-matched and marked EXCERPT in the registry. Proposed: allow this for sections over about 20,000 characters, with the registry recording the excerpt boundaries and the whole-section character count [ID-gaps 4].
5. **Rule 19 / batteries: a whole-chapter "waive" screen before any "only waiver ban" claim.** Battery H4-B40's waiver patterns missed "a provision by which the tenant waives" (Idaho Code § 55-2706(5)). Proposed: an absence claim of the form "the only X is Y" also runs the bare stem ("waive", "form", "carbon monoxide") over the titles named, and reads every hit [ID-gaps 5].

- [H2] Battery H2-B14 (services 'not provided' wording) passed its positives but could not match the floating-home rule 'No fees may be charged except for rent, services and utilities actually provided' (Idaho Code § 55-2708(5)), which was found by reading Idaho Code § 55-2708 for another topic. H2-B33 (fees ... 'actually|not provided') was run with that section as a real positive. Lesson: for 'fee for a service not provided' absences, search the affirmative form ('except for ... actually provided') as well as the negative.
- [H5] Rule 36 boundary for this batch: topics whose regime could live in IDAPA were answered as statutory absences with the boundary named in body and notes (lead, asbestos, pesticide, certificate of occupancy, sprinkler, inspection orders, energy data), relying on the main pass's IDAPA chapter-title screen (REGISTRY.tsv). Only one fetch was filed (IDAPA 58.01.08 for private-well-testing), because only there does a statute (Idaho Code § 39-103(12)) point to a rule set that would change the answer.
- [H8] animal-care-access-vi tagged ID as the tenant's advance consent with an officer fallback; it is consistent with the proposed Idaho pet-policy-id override (no landlord seizure of a pet except through court; report to animal control), since under Idaho Code § 25-3511 the duty to take a neglected or abandoned animal belongs to 'any law enforcement officer or animal care and control officer'. The main pass should check that the override's wording and this tag do not contradict (the tag lets a tenant-named person in, which is consent, not seizure).

### Proposed topic questions
- **Lease with an option to buy (proposed topic key `lease-option-disclosure`).** Idaho's Property Condition Disclosure Act reaches 'a lease with an option to purchase' of one to four dwelling units (Idaho Code § 55-2504). The form is due within ten days of accepting the offer (Idaho Code § 55-2509), and there is a three-business-day rescission (Idaho Code § 55-2515). Nebraska, South Carolina and North Carolina raised the same point. For now `edu-required-disclosures-id` carries it.

## Shared edit: parking tag charge (Claude Code, 2026-11-12)

Taylor's decision (2026-11-12) on the gaps log §6 proposal, merged centrally under rule 62 because it only narrows what Landlord may charge. In `parking-vehicle-rules-id` (ID, IA, NM), the sentence that let Landlord charge "the cost" of tags, decals and access cards now reads: "Landlord ... may issue parking tags, decals, or access devices (such as cards, fobs, or garage or gate remotes). Tenant will pay $[amount; enter $0 if there is no charge] for each one Landlord issues, and $[amount; enter $0 if there is no charge] for each one that is lost, damaged other than by ordinary wear, or not returned when the tenancy ends; Landlord will not charge any other amount for them." (Revised the same day at Taylor's request: separate amounts for an issued device and a replacement, so a first one can be free; "access devices" so a garage remote is covered.)

- **Why:** Idaho Code § 55-305(2)(a) bars charging an amount "greater than that agreed upon in the rental agreement"; an unstated cost isn't an agreed amount. Taylor chose a stated price over the gaps log's free-tags wording so each landlord decides whether to charge, and the tenant sees the price before signing. Any charge must still be reasonable (§ 55-305(1)).
- **Closes** item 4 of the s10 open list above for this row (`keys` and `smoking-policy` were already replaced for Idaho by `keys-id` and `smoking-policy-id` at the gaps sync).
- **Checked against IA and NM** (their logs carry the same note): neither state's note on this row is affected, since both rest on towing statutes.
